Hmm, the issue is that dllseg with t=x and u=x->next tries to describe a segment from x to x->next with x as the last node. But x is the FIRST node, not the last (in a multi-node list). The t=yp parameter is supposed to be the last node, but here t=x is actually the first.

Wait, let me re-read the dllseg definition:
```
dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || 
  exists z, data_at(field_addr(x, next), struct list*, z) * 
            data_at(field_addr(x, prev), struct list*, xp) * 
            dllseg_shape(z, x, yp, y)
```

This expands from the LEFT (head) towards the right. So for `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)`:
- Unfold x: data_at(x->next, next_val) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(next_val, x, t, u)
- Unfold next_val: data_at(next_val->next, n2) * data_at(next_val->prev, x) * dllseg_shape(n2, next_val, t, u)
- ...
- Eventually we reach a point where the head equals u or something.

Actually, `dllseg_shape` keeps the yp fixed and expands the head. So when the head becomes equal to y (u), it terminates. In each step, we expand the leftmost node.

For pre_loop: t=x, u=y_289
- `dllseg_shape(x, 0, x, y_289)`:

  - But z == y ? y_289 == y_289. But xp == yp? x == x? Yes.
  - So dllseg_shape(y_289, x, x, y_289) where x == y_289 → terminates with emp.
  
  So the expansion is: data_at(x->next, y_289) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * emp
  
  This describes just the head node x with its prev=0 and next=y_289. That's exactly right! In RP_pre_loop, t=x, u=x->next, and the segment should cover just x.

So: data_at(x->next, x->next) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * emp = data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0)

This is correct for RP_pre_loop! The last node t = x has t->next = x->next = u.

At iter_2: dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) where t = first_node, t->next = second_node
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, first_node, second_node):
  - Expand x: data_at(x->next, n1) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(n1, x, first_node, second_node)
  - Expand n1: data_at(n1->next, n2) * data_at(n1->prev, x) * dllseg_shape(n2, n1, first_node, second_node)
  - But n1 IS first_node (= t), and n1->next = n2 = second_node (t->next)
  - dllseg_shape(n2, n1, first_node, second_node): n2 == second_node? And n1 == first_node? Yes → emp

So: data_at(x->next, t) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * data_at(t->next, u) * data_at(t->prev, x) * emp

Combined with dlistrep_shape(u, t): data_at(u->next, ...) * data_at(u->prev, t) * ...

Total: data_at(x->next, t) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * data_at(t->next, u) * data_at(t->prev, x) * dlistrep_shape(u, t)

This is RP_iter_2 exactly!

So the invariant with field-expressions:
```

```

But this has `t->next` appearing twice — once in dllseg_shape and once in dlistrep_shape. Wouldn't that cause dup_data_at?

In dllseg_shape, `t->next` is the y parameter (endpoint). The expansion produces data_at(t->next, ...) for the field. In dlistrep_shape(t->next, t), t->next is the first argument (start), so the unfold produces data_at(t->next, ...) too.

BUT: dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) produces data_at(t->next, ...): the data_at for t->next is produced as part of expanding the last node t inside the segment. It shows t->next = u (the endpoint). And dlistrep_shape(u, t) starts with u as head. So both point to u's data_at.

But wait, they DON'T produce the same data_at. dllseg_shape produces data_at(t->next, u) as part of t's node fields. dlistrep_shape(u, t) produces data_at(u->next, ...) and data_at(u->prev, t) — different addresses! So no dup_data_at.

Hmm actually, let me re-check. `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next)` with y = t->next:

  - But next_val = t->next, and y = t->next. So next_val == y.
  - dllseg_shape(next_val, t, yp, y): next_val == y means yes, t == yp means yes → emp

    This infinite expansion is wrong!

- If l = 0: emp
- If l != 0: data_at(l->next, ...) * data_at(l->prev, p) * dlistrep_shape(..., l)

So `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)` unfolds to: data_at(t->next->next, ...) * data_at(t->next->prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(..., t->next)

This does NOT give data_at for t->next directly. It gives data_at for (t->next)->next and (t->next)->prev. So the field expression t->next only serves as a NODE POINTER, not as a direct data_at.

So using t->next as the first arg of dlistrep doesn't create data_at(&(t->next), ...), it creates data_at for (t->next)->next and (t->next)->prev.

- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next): t->next is the endpoint. The dllseg expansion reaches t and creates data_at(t->next, ...) which is data_at(&(t->next), struct list*, t->next_val).
- dlistrep_shape(u, t): u is the start. Unfolds to data_at(u->next, ...) * data_at(u->prev, t) * ...

But wait, these are the SAME value: t->next = u. However, the data_at from dllseg expansion is at address &(t->next), and the data_at from dlistrep are at &(u->next) and &(u->prev). Different addresses, no dup.

But what about the field expression in dllseg? QCP evaluates `t->next` in dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) and creates data_at(&(t->next), ...). When dllseg is later unfolded, it ALSO creates data_at(&(t->next), ...). Same address, POSSIBLE dup.

With invariant: `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)`

After t = u: t is now old_u. The dllseg_shape(x, 0, old_u, old_u->next): 
- x != old_u->next: unfold from x... eventually reach old_u: data_at(old_u->next, old_un) * data_at(old_u->prev, old_uprev) * dllseg_shape(old_un, old_u, old_u, old_u->next)
- dllseg_shape(old_un, old_u, old_u, old_u->next): old_un = old_u->next, y = old_u->next. old_un == old_u->next (yes). xp = old_u == yp = old_u (yes). → emp.

So dllseg_shape(x, 0, old_u, old_u->next) expands to include old_u->next = old_u_next.

After u = t->next: u = (new_t)->next = old_u->next. The assignment reads t->next which is old_u->next. The data_at for this is exposed by dllseg expansion. Good!

But wait, `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next)` has t->next as a field expression. When QCP evaluates the field expression during the invariant establishment, it might create a separate data_at for t->next. When strategies later unfold dllseg, they also create data_at for t->next. This could be dup.

For a backward traversal (p = p->prev):

Here:
- dllseg: segment from head to p, with p->prev as the last node in segment. 
  - p->prev is the node BEFORE p in the list.
  - The segment covers from head to (but not including) p.
  - p->prev is the last node before p. p->prev->next = p.
- dlistrep: tail from p->next to end.

But the example says "both arguments are C field expressions that re-evaluate automatically after p = p->prev." So the predicates RE-EVALUATE with the new p value. The key insight is that p->prev and p->next create data_at for p's fields, making them directly accessible.

So in this approach:
- dllseg_shape creates data_at for p->prev (as part of the segment endpoint)
- dlistrep_shape creates data_at for p->next (as the start of the tail)
- Together, they cover p's fields (prev and next)
- p's own data_at is IMPLICIT, not explicit

This matches the desired: dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, p_prev) with p_prev = old_u and u = new_u.

This invariant works WITHOUT t! The variable t is used in the loop body but not in the invariant. Wait, but the post-loop code uses t:
```
t->next = y;

    y->prev = t;
}
```

t is used after the loop. But t is NOT in the invariant. After loop exit (u = 0):
Invariant: dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(0, p_prev) → dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, 0)

But t is a separate variable. How does QCP know t's value? Before the loop, t was set to x. In the loop, t was updated to u (before u was updated to t->next). After the loop, t = last non-null node, and we access t->next.

If t is not in the invariant, QCP can't find t at the post-loop! "cannot find program variable t" error.

So t MUST be in the invariant for the post-loop too. 

What if t = p_prev? At pre_loop, t = x. p_prev = 0 (exists). They're different. But after one iteration, t = u_old and p_prev should be t_old (which becomes the prev of u_old). Actually, at the loop head, t is always one node behind p_prev? Or ahead?

Let me map the variables at each RP:

RP_pre_loop:
- State: dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, p_prev)
- From the actual states: t = x, u = y_289 (x->next)
- For the invariant to match: dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, u) with u = x->next.
  - Expand: data_at(x->next, x->next) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * emp
  - Here, the yp parameter is p_prev. In the expansion, yp = x. So p_prev = x.
  - But t = x, so p_prev = t!

RP_iter_2:
- t = y_289, u = y_297
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, u) with u = y_297
  - Expand: data_at(x->next, y_289) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(y_289, x, p_prev, y_297)

RP_iter_3:

The difference is that in the u-centered form, t appears in the invariant AS p_prev (which equals t). But in the Inv Assert, if we write `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, u) * ...`, QCP sees p_prev as an exists variable, not t. And t is NOT in the invariant.

Here is the summary:

## Result

**QCP_VERIFIED: NO** -- 4 admitted witness goals remain after all retries.

## Analysis

This is a DLL forward-traversal loop that finds the tail of list `x` and appends list `y`. The loop is `t = u; u = t->next` with condition `while(u)`.

### What was tried

The invariant `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)` correctly captures the state (verified by diff analysis against all 3 recording points). However, it fails in two ways:

1. **Without PROP**: `Assign Exec fail` at `t->next = y` (line 22) -- after loop exit (`u == 0`), the state `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, 0)` cannot be unfolded by field-write strategies to expose `data_at(&(t->next), ...)`.

2. **With `t->next == u` PROP**: Symexec completes (0 fatal errors), but 4 witness goals remain Admitted: `entail_wit_1` (entry), `entail_wit_2` (preservation), `return_wit_1`, `return_wit_2`. The PROP expression `t->next == u` creates a `data_at` for `t->next` (fixing the assign), but creates proof obligations between the PROP and the SEP predicates that strategies cannot discharge.

### Fix strategies attempted

| Strategy | Variant | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Original (no PROP) | `dllseg_shape(x,0,t,u) * dlistrep_shape(u,t)` | Fatal: Assign Exec fail |
| Strategy 4 (non-null PROP) | `x != 0` + original | Fatal: Assign Exec fail |
| Field exp in dlistrep | `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)` variant | Fatal: Memory Read |
| Field exp in dllseg | `dllseg_shape(x,0,t,t->next)` variant | 4 admits |
| Exists + dlistrep at t | `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(x,0,p_prev,t) * dlistrep_shape(t,p_prev)` | Fatal: cannot find u |
| Full dlistrep | `dlistrep_shape(x,0) * dlistrep_shape(y,0)` | Fatal: cannot find u |
| PROP + non-null | `x != 0 && t->next == u` + original | 4 admits |
| PROP + more non-null | `x != 0 && t != 0 && t->next == u` + original | 4 admits |

The fundamental challenge: `u` must be in the invariant (for the loop condition), `t` must also be in the invariant (for post-loop `t->next = y`), but the strategies cannot simultaneously handle field access at the inner boundary node `t` of `dllseg` while maintaining the `t->next == u` relation.

### Output files

- Invariant file: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260523_091840/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/append/tmp/app_inv.c`
- Workspace (states, unrolled, logs): `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260523_091840/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/append/workspace/`
- Result: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/test_results_20260523_091840/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/append/result.txt`

Actually, from SKILL.md: "`/*@ Inv Assert ... */` **replaces** the entire program state at the loop head." This means the invariant REPLACES everything, and anything not in the invariant is DROPPED.

So the entailment: state |- invariant means: the state must imply the invariant, and extra parts of the state are lost.

For: data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, x) |- dlistrep_shape(u, x)

This is NOT valid! The two data_at are lost. Unless the strategies can somehow consume them.

But maybe the strategies HANDLE THIS by using the `t->prev` and `u == t->next` field expressions:
- `t->prev` is a field expression in dllseg_shape → QCP creates data_at for it
- `u == t->next` in PROP → QCP creates data_at for t->next

At assertion establishment:
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) = dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, x):

  - dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, x): evaluates to emp.

- dlistrep_shape(u, t) = dlistrep_shape(u, x): tail unchanged.

- u == t->next: checks u = x->next. Creates data_at(&(x->next), u, struct list*).

So the invariant in internal form would be:
data_at(&(x->prev), 0, struct list*) *    // from t->prev field expression
data_at(&(x->next), u, struct list*) *    // from u == t->next PROP
emp *                                      // from dllseg_shape(x,0,0,x)
dlistrep_shape(u, x) *                     // from dlistrep_shape(u, t)
dlistrep_shape(y, 0)                       // from dlistrep_shape(y, 0)

This matches the precondition! data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, x) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)

So the field expressions CREATE the data_at that would otherwise be lost! That's the key insight I missed.

At pre_loop (t=x):
- t->prev = x->prev = 0: field expression creates data_at(x->prev, 0)
- u == t->next: creates data_at(x->next, u)
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, x) = emp
- dlistrep_shape(u, x) covers the tail

Total invariant = data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, x) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)

This matches the precondition exactly!

And after iterations, when t moves forward:
- t->prev changes, creating a new data_at for the current t's prev
- dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) expands to include nodes from x to t
- dlistrep_shape(u, t) covers the tail from u = t->next

The field expressions on BOTH sides (t->prev in dllseg, u implicitly via PROP, and dlistrep) create the necessary data_at to make the invariant self-consistent.

This is exactly strategy 3 from SKILL.md: "Widen the boundary with field expressions on both sides."

1. I tried `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) * dlistrep_shape(u, t)` — worked for entry/preservation but failed at post-loop assign.

2. I considered the field expression approach but worried about dup_data_at. I tried:
   - `dlistrep_shape(t->next, t)` — Memory Read error (circular dependency, t->next needs data_at to evaluate the invariant itself)

4. I tried `exists p_prev, dllseg_shape(x, 0, p_prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(t, p_prev)` — fatal error: cannot find u.

5. I did NOT try `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)` because I thought:
   - At pre_loop (t=x), t->prev=0, dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, x) = emp
   - x's head node wouldn't be covered
   - The head node's data_at would be lost

My mistake was: I didn't realize that the field expressions `t->prev` and `u == t->next` (in PROP) would CREATE data_at for the head node's fields, compensating for the empty dllseg at pre_loop.

The SKILL.md guidance on field expressions says:
"A C field expression in a predicate argument (p->prev, t->next) creates an explicit data_at for that field."

And strategy 3 says: "Use field expressions on BOTH sides to explicitly expose the gap node's fields — the boundary becomes self-adapting and no strategy rule is needed at all."

I read this but didn't fully apply it. I interpreted "both sides" as needing both dllseg and dlistrep to have field expressions, which I thought would cause dup_data_at. The correct interpretation is: use field expressions for DIFFERENT fields (t->prev in dllseg, t->next via u==t->next PROP for access), avoiding dup.

The issue with my SKILL.md reading was:
1. I was overly cautious about dup_data_at
2. I didn't fully internalize that field expressions in the invariant CREATE data_at that fills gaps in coverage
3. I didn't think to combine `t->prev` field expression with `u == t->next` PROP

Let me now address the user's question directly: why didn't I try strategy 4, and is there an issue with SKILL.md guidance?

Actually, the correct answer uses strategy 3 (field expressions on both sides) combined with strategy 4 (non-null PROP `t != 0`). The user asked about strategy 4 specifically.

Strategy 4 says: "Add non-null PROP to trigger unfolds: If entail or safety witnesses fail after symexec passes, strategies may need explicit `x != 0` in PROP to fire unfold rules for `dlistrep_shape(x, ...)`."

I DID try strategy 4: I added `x != 0` to the original invariant, and later `x != 0 && t != 0`. But with the original invariant structure, these didn't help because the problem wasn't about triggering unfolds — it was about the structure of the invariant making t->next inaccessible at post-loop.

The issue with the SKILL.md guidance: strategy 4 assumes you already have the correct invariant STRUCTURE and just need to add non-null triggers. But in my case, the STRUCTURE was wrong (using dllseg with simple variables t,u instead of field expressions t->prev). No amount of non-null PROP would fix that.

The guidance could be improved by clarifying that strategy 4 (non-null PROP) only works when the invariant already has the correct field-expression structure, and that strategy 3 (field expressions on both sides) should be tried FIRST for post-loop assign issues.

Also, the SKILL.md guidance on field expressions says they cause dup_data_at with predicates. But the correct answer uses field expressions in dllseg (t->prev) and PROP (u == t->next) for DIFFERENT fields — avoiding dup. The guidance could clarify that field expressions for DIFFERENT fields (prev vs next) don't conflict.

[TEXT]
你问得好。让我诚实分析为什么我没有尝试这个解答，以及 SKILL.md 指引存在的问题。

## 为什么没试 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)`

我的推理链条中有一个关键盲点：

**我担心在 pre_loop（t=x, t->prev=0）时，`dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, x)` = emp，x 头节点的 data_at 会丢失。** 但我忽略了：`t->prev` 是一个 **field expression**，它在 Inv Assert 建立时会**直接创建** `data_at(&(x->prev), 0)`。同理 `u == t->next` 在 PROP 中也会创建 `data_at(&(x->next), u)`。这两个 field expression 自动填补了 dllseg 收缩为 emp 时留下的空隙。

正确解答的内在工作原理：

```
pre_loop (t=x, u=x->next):
  dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)  → dllseg_shape(x, 0, 0, x) → emp
  u == t->next                    → 创建 data_at(&(x->next), u)
  t->prev field expr              → 创建 data_at(&(x->prev), 0)
  dlistrep_shape(u, t)            → dlistrep_shape(x->next, x) 覆盖尾部
  
  合起来: data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(u, x) ✓

iter_2 (t=y_289, u=y_297):
  dllseg_shape(x, 0, y_289->prev, y_289)
    → x != y_289, 展开: data_at(x->next, y_289) * data_at(x->prev, 0) 
      * dllseg_shape(y_289, x, x, y_289)
    → y_289->prev = x, yp=x, y=y_289, xp==yp? x==x → emp
    → data_at(x->next, t) * data_at(x->prev, 0) [覆盖 x 头节点]
  u == t->next → data_at(&(t->next), u)
  dlistrep_shape(u, t) → 展开: data_at(u->next, ...) * data_at(u->prev, t) * ...
  
  合起来: data_at(x->next, t) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * data_at(t->next, u) 
          * data_at(t->prev, x) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) ✓ 与 RP_iter_2 完全匹配
```

**field expression 在不同节点展开时自动产生不同的 data_at 地址**——如果 dllseg 的展开已经产生某个 data_at，那么 field expression 产生的 data_at 会被策略合并而不是视为 dup。这就是策略3（"widen the boundary"）的精髓。

## SKILL.md 指引的问题

关于策略4（"Add non-null PROP to trigger unfolds"），我**确实试了**——在原不变式加了 `x != 0`，后来加了 `x != 0 && t != 0`。都不起作用。但这不是策略4本身的问题，而是**前置条件不满足**：

| SKILL.md 策略 | 前提假设 | 我当时的状态 |
|---|---|---|
| 策略4: 加 non-null PROP 触发 unfold | 不变式**结构已正确**，只是策略缺少 unfold 触发条件 | 不变式结构是 `dllseg_shape(x,0,t,u)`，t 在边界内部而非边界上，**结构本身不对** |

SKILL.md 的策略列表是**优先级顺序**，但隐含了一个假设：前面的策略已经让不变式结构正确了。当策略1-3（结构调整）还没到位时，策略4（调整 PROP 触发条件）自然无效。

**SKILL.md 可以改进的地方：**

1. **策略3的说明不够具体。** "Widen the boundary with field expressions on both sides" 没有明确解释：关键是用**不同的 field expression** 分别处理 `next` 和 `prev` 字段，且 field expression 自动产生 data_at 填补覆盖空隙。

2. **策略4应该加一句前提。** 类似 "Only effective when the invariant structure already exposes the relevant nodes at predicate boundaries." 否则会误导读者在不正确的结构上加 non-null PROP。

3. **缺少对 "field expression creates data_at" 的强调。** 这个机制是理解策略3工作的关键——field expression 不仅仅是参数的另一种写法，它会**主动在 assertion state 中产生 data_at**，从而不需要策略规则就能暴露字段。

Same 4 admits. `t->next` appearing in both dllseg and PROP causes the same issue — both create data_at for the same address, the strategies can't fully resolve the resulting proof obligations.

The working version uses `t->prev` (a DIFFERENT field) in dllseg and `t->next == u` in PROP — two different field expressions at two different addresses, no overlap.

Let me also try without `u == t->next` in PROP (just `t != 0`), using `t->prev` in dllseg and `t->next` in dlistrep:

`dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(t->next, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)`

But this doesn't have u... The loop condition needs u.

Let me try: `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t) * dlistrep_shape(u, t) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0)` with PROP `u == t->next && t != 0`.

This has t->prev in dllseg and t->next in PROP — two different fields, same as the working version, except the SEP order is different.

[TEXT]
两个带 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` 和 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next)` 的版本都是 4 admits。关键差异不是位置或 PROP 本身，而是哪个 field expression 出现在 SEP 中。让我验证一个版本：用 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)`，但 SEP 顺序与正确答案不同。

0 admits! SEP order doesn't matter. The key is `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)` with `u == t->next && t != 0` in PROP.

So the conclusion is clear: the critical difference is `t->prev` vs `t`/`u`/`t->next` as the yp parameter in dllseg_shape. Using `t->prev` (a different field expression than `t->next` in PROP) avoids creating data_at for the same field in two places.

Now let me update the result.

[TEXT]
**0 admits.** 顺序无关，关键是 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)` 中的 field expression `t->prev` 和 PROP `u == t->next` 中的 `t->next` 是**两个不同的堆字段**（prev vs next），各自在 assertion state 中产生独立的 data_at，不会冲突。

而 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` 内部隐含 `t->next == u`，再加上 PROP `u == t->next` 显式声明 `t->next`，同一个地址 `&(t->next)` 出现了两个 data_at 来源。

恢复正确答案，更新 result：

[TEXT]
**结论：** 用 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t->prev, t)` 配合 `u == t->next && t != 0` 即可通过，无论 SEP 顺序如何。`t->prev` 和 `t->next` 是两个不同的 field，各自产生独立的 data_at，不会 dup。而 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` 或 `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, t->next)` 都会让 `t->next` 在 SEP 和 PROP 中重复出现，导致策略无法消解 4 个 admit。
